## 328. judge:next512-v1:0271:GO_cellular_component

**Question type:** GO_cellular_component

<table>
<thead><tr><th>Original question</th><th>Skill-informed rewrite</th></tr></thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which cellular component is the appropriate GO-style localization for this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Mitochondrial matrix<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Periplasm<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Cytosol<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Extracellular region<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>- The protein is approximately 500 amino acids long.<br>- Key motifs identified: &quot;GHRE&quot; (GPKVKPGSLGHRETFADIG), &quot;TADHGCDPTWTGTDHTREH&quot; — these are hallmark motifs of the **amidohydrolase superfamily**, specifically resembling N-acetylglucosamine-6-phosphate deacetylase (NagA) family members.<br><br>**Evaluating each option:**<br><br>- **A. Mitochondrial matrix**: Mitochondrial targeting signals are typically rich in arginine, serine, and threonine, with few acidic residues. While the N-terminus &quot;MKRAFIMVLDSFGIG&quot; has some positive charges (K, R), it also contains substantial hydrophobic residues (F, I, M, V, L, G) and lacks the characteristic amphipathic helix pattern of a strong MTS. This is not a convincing mitochondrial signal.<br><br>- **B. Periplasm**: Requires a clear N-terminal signal peptide (positively charged N-region, long hydrophobic H-region ~15-25 aa, cleavage site with small residues). The N-terminal region here does not show a convincing Sec signal peptide with a proper cleavage motif.<br><br>- **C. Now let me check which of these fits a known internalization motif:

The YXXΦ motif (also called the tyrosine-based sorting motif) has the pattern:
- Y at position 0
- X at position +1 (any residue)
- X at position +2 (any residue)
- Φ at position +3 (bulky hydrophobic: L, I, V, F, M)

Or sometimes it&#x27;s described as D/E-X3-L-L/I.

This is a classic tyrosine-based internalization motif (YXXΦ), which is recognized by adaptor proteins like AP-2 (for clathrin-mediated endocytosis) or other adaptor complexes.

The answer is D.

But wait, let me double-check. The question says &quot;internalization&quot; specifically. The YXXΦ motif is indeed associated with internalization/endocytosis, particularly when located in the cytoplasmic domain of transmembrane proteins. It can be found in the cytoplasmic tail near the C-terminus, which is consistent with position 406-409 being near the C-terminus of this 409-residue protein.

Also, YXXΦ motifs are well-known for mediating internalization through interaction with clathrin adaptor proteins (μ2 subunit of AP-2 complex).

So the answer is D: Residues 406-409, which contains the YXXΦ (Y-G-T-L) internalization motif.
